\section{Finite potentials and continuous sequential regularization}
\label{sec:intrinsic-psh-continuation-seven}

The cones and function spaces in this section belong to the original
multiprojective model $\mathcal X$. The results apply without a countability,
normality or unibranchness assumption. They address finite potentials and
continuous regularization; they do not identify the model cone with an
intrinsic numerical cone, or establish PSH2 or sequential regularization
for arbitrary singular potentials.

\begin{lemma}[Finite rational FS functions]
\label{lem:seven-finite-rational-fs}
\uses{def:gl-fs}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsRatFS.isFinFS_of_everywhere_ne_bot}
\leanok
Let $f\in\mathcal H^{\mathrm{gf}}_{\mathbb Q}(\mathcal X,a)$.
If $f(x)>-\infty$ for every $x\in\mathcal X^{\mathrm{an}}$, then
$f\in\mathcal H_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal X,a)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose the original finite section family representing $f$. Its weights
are rational. At any point where all sections vanish, every summand has
value $-\infty$, hence so does their maximum and its positive multiple.
Thus at every point at least one section is nonzero. The same original
family is base point free, as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Everywhere finite potentials detect the model nef cone]
\label{thm:seven-finite-psh-nef}
\uses{lem:seven-finite-rational-fs, cor:gl-pshhom, lem:gl-cones,
thm:gl-fs-system-theory}
\source{fhx-envelope-20261028:prop:BJ-package, bj22-trivval:defi:psh}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsPSH.exists_isFinFS_net_of_everywhere_ne_bot,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsPSH.mem_nefCone_of_everywhere_ne_bot,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.mem_nefCone_iff_exists_everywhere_finite_psh}
\leanok
A real model class $\theta$ belongs to $N(\mathcal X)$ if and only if it
admits an everywhere finite $\theta$-psh potential. Moreover, for an
everywhere finite $u\in\mathrm{PSH}(\mathcal X,\theta)$, the original
rational FS regularization net consists of finite rational FS functions.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $u=\inf_i f_i$ be its decreasing rational FS regularization,
with classes $a_i\to\theta$. Since $u\le f_i$, each $f_i$ is everywhere
greater than $-\infty$. The preceding lemma gives
$f_i\in\mathcal H_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal X,a_i)$, so
$a_i\in C(\mathcal X)$. Taking the limit gives
$\theta\in\overline{C(\mathcal X)}=N(\mathcal X)$.
Conversely, for a class in $N(\mathcal X)$, the constant zero is a
$\theta$-psh function and is everywhere finite. Psh functions never take
the value $+\infty$, so ``everywhere greater than $-\infty$'' is equivalent
to everywhere finite here.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[PSH2 for everywhere finite limits]
\label{thm:seven-finite-limit-psh2}
\uses{thm:seven-finite-psh-nef, thm:gl-fs-system-theory, lem:gl-cones}
\source{fhx-envelope-20261028:prop:BJ-package, bj22-trivval:thm:psh12}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.isPSH_of_antitone_of_everywhere_ne_bot}
\leanok
Let $(u_i)$ be a decreasing directed net with
$u_i\in\mathrm{PSH}(\mathcal X,\theta_i)$ and $\theta_i\to\theta$.
If $w=\inf_i u_i$ is everywhere greater than $-\infty$, then
$w\in\mathrm{PSH}(\mathcal X,\theta)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The inequality $w\le u_i$ implies that every $u_i$ is everywhere finite.
The preceding theorem gives $\theta_i\in N(\mathcal X)$ for every $i$.
Closedness of the model nef cone yields $\theta\in N(\mathcal X)$.
The independently proved nef decreasing-limit theorem therefore applies.
The limit is nontrivial since it is finite at the trivial point.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Continuous sequential rational regularization]
\label{thm:seven-continuous-rational-fs-sequence}
\uses{thm:seven-finite-psh-nef, cor:gl-pshhom}
\source{fhx-envelope-20261028:prop:BJ-package, bj22-trivval:defi:psh}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsPSH.exists_isFinFS_seq_of_continuous}
\leanok
Let $u\colon\mathcal X^{\mathrm{an}}\to\mathbb R$ be continuous and
$\theta$-psh. There are rational classes $a_n\to\theta$ and decreasing
$f_n\in\mathcal H_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal X,a_n)$ such that, for every
$n$ and $x$,
\[
 \|a_n-\theta\|<\frac1{n+1},\qquad
 u(x)\le f_n(x)<u(x)+\frac1{n+1}.
\]
In particular $u=\inf_n f_n$, and the convergence is uniform. No
countability assumption on the coefficient field or analytic space is needed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Take the decreasing finite rational FS net $f_i$ with classes
$a_i\to\theta$ and infimum $u$. For each $n$, the open sets
$\{x:f_i(x)<u(x)+(n+1)^{-1}\}$ cover the compact analytic space:
pointwise covering follows from the infimum formula, and openness from
continuity of the upper bound and of every FS function. They form a
directed increasing cover because the FS net is decreasing. Compactness
therefore provides one index $j_n$ for which the strict inequality holds
everywhere. Class convergence provides an index $k_n$ such that
$\|a_i-\theta\|<(n+1)^{-1}$ for $i\ge k_n$.
Choose $i_0\ge j_0,k_0$, and recursively choose
$i_{n+1}\ge i_n,j_{n+1},k_{n+1}$ using directedness. Set $f_n=f_{i_n}$
and $a_n=a_{i_n}$. These functions decrease, and the two stated estimates
hold by construction. The estimates imply convergence of the classes and
uniform convergence of the functions. Since the sequence decreases and
has pointwise limit $u$, its pointwise infimum is $u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Prescribed dyadic classes]
\label{cor:seven-continuous-dyadic-rational-fs}
\uses{thm:seven-continuous-rational-fs-sequence, lem:gl-fs-system}
\source{fhx-envelope-20261028:prop:BJ-package, bj22-trivval:defi:psh}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsPSH.exists_isFinFS_seq_dyadic_of_continuous}
\leanok
If $\theta\in\mathbb Q^r$ and $u$ is a continuous real-valued
$\theta$-psh potential, then there is a decreasing sequence
$f_n\in\mathcal H_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal X,\theta+2^{-n}\mathbf1)$
with $u=\inf_n f_n$ and
$u(x)\le f_n(x)<u(x)+(n+1)^{-1}$ for all $n,x$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Take the classes $a_m$ and functions $g_m$ of the preceding theorem.
For each $n$, convergence $a_m\to\theta$ implies
$a_m\le\theta+2^{-n}\mathbf1$ for every sufficiently large $m$.
Recursively choose a strictly increasing sequence $\varphi(n)$ meeting
these bounds. In particular $\varphi(n)\ge n$.
Set $f_n=g_{\varphi(n)}$. Class monotonicity promotes each function,
without changing its values, to the prescribed class
$\theta+2^{-n}\mathbf1$. The functions decrease because $g_m$ decreases.
The original uniform estimate and $\varphi(n)\ge n$ give the stated
uniform estimate. For every $m$, the inequality
$\inf_n f_n\le g_{\varphi(m)}\le g_m$ proves
$\inf_n f_n\le\inf_m g_m=u$; the reverse inequality follows from
$u\le g_m$. Thus the pointwise infimum is $u$.
\end{proof}

\section{Support-trivial tests for singular decreasing limits}

This continuation uses the actual original multiprojective model and the
trivial valuation of the residue field at each of its original scheme
supports. Unlike the everywhere-finite checkpoint, it permits the limit
to have value $-\infty$ at other analytic points. It does not establish
full PSH2 or identify model psh functions with intrinsic BJ psh functions.

\begin{definition}[Original support-trivial point]
\label{def:seven-original-support-trivial-point}
\uses{def:gl-points}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsMultigraded.zeroScale,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsNormalized.zeroScale,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.supportTrivialPoint,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.supportTrivialPoint_supp}
\leanok
For $x\in\mathcal X^{\mathrm{an}}$, let $\zeta_x$ be the seminorm on the
SAME original model ring given by $|p|_{\zeta_x}=0$ when $|p|_x=0$ and
$|p|_{\zeta_x}=1$ otherwise.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
The existing zero-scaling construction gives a multiplicative seminorm,
trivial on the original coefficient field. If the value of $p$ at $x$
vanishes, the value of each homogeneous component vanishes by the original
multigradedness inequality; otherwise the value of $p$ at $\zeta_x$ is one,
and bounds every component. Thus the new seminorm is multigraded. For each
coordinate group, some original coordinate has value one at $x$ by
normalization, so also has value one at $\zeta_x$. All coordinates have
value at most one there. Hence the new seminorm is normalized and belongs
to the original model analytic space. Its kernel is exactly the original
kernel, so its original algebraic support is unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Every original FS pole is detected at its support]
\label{lem:seven-original-fs-support-trivial-poles}
\uses{def:seven-original-support-trivial-point, def:gl-fs, lem:gl-sections}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.secAbs_supportTrivialPoint_eq_zero_iff,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.FSData.eval_supportTrivialPoint_eq_bot_iff}
\leanok
For every original model section $s$, its norm is zero at $\zeta_x$ if and
only if it is zero at $x$. Every original FS datum $D$ therefore satisfies
$D(\zeta_x)=-\infty$ if and only if $D(x)=-\infty$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The original section norm is a finite maximum of original homogeneous
numerator values after one admissible denominator clearing. A finite
maximum of nonnegative numbers is zero exactly when all terms are zero.
Every numerator has the same vanishing condition at $x$ and $\zeta_x$,
because the two seminorms have the same kernel. This proves the first
claim. A finite FS datum has value $-\infty$ exactly when all of its
original sections vanish. Apply the first claim to every section of the
SAME original finite family to obtain the second.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Support-trivial finiteness forces nefness]
\label{thm:seven-support-trivial-psh-nef}
\uses{lem:seven-original-fs-support-trivial-poles, cor:gl-pshhom,
lem:gl-cones}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.IsPSH.mem_nefCone_of_supportTrivialPoint_ne_bot}
\leanok
If $u\in\mathrm{PSH}(\mathcal X,\theta)$ and
$u(\zeta_x)>-\infty$ for every original $x$, then $\theta\in N(\mathcal X)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Take the original decreasing rational FS net $u=\inf_i f_i$ with classes
$a_i\to\theta$. At each $\zeta_x$ the inequality $u\le f_i$ shows that
$f_i$ is greater than $-\infty$. The preceding pole test shows that $f_i$
is greater than $-\infty$ at every original analytic $x$. Its SAME rational
FS data is therefore base point free and belongs to
$\mathcal H_{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal X,a_i)$, whence
$a_i\in C(\mathcal X)$. Taking the limit in the closed model nef cone gives
$\theta\in N(\mathcal X)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[PSH2 with support-trivial finite limits]
\label{thm:seven-support-trivial-limit-psh2}
\uses{thm:seven-support-trivial-psh-nef, thm:gl-fs-system-theory,
lem:gl-cones}
\source{fhx-envelope-20261028:prop:BJ-package, bj22-trivval:thm:psh12}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.isPSH_of_antitone_of_supportTrivialPoint_ne_bot}
\leanok
Let $u_i\in\mathrm{PSH}(\mathcal X,\theta_i)$ be decreasing with
$\theta_i\to\theta$, and let $w=\inf_i u_i$. If
$w(\zeta_x)>-\infty$ for every original $x$, then
$w\in\mathrm{PSH}(\mathcal X,\theta)$, with no unibranchness hypothesis.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For every $i,x$, the inequality $w(\zeta_x)\le u_i(\zeta_x)$ implies
that $u_i(\zeta_x)>-\infty$. The preceding theorem gives
$\theta_i\in N(\mathcal X)$, so closedness gives
$\theta\in N(\mathcal X)$. The limit is nontrivial, since it is finite at
$\zeta_{\star}$. Apply the independently proved nef PSH2 to the SAME original
net and limit. No homogeneity or envelope property of the limit is assumed.
\end{proof}

\section{The genuine native support-trivial residue valuation}

\begin{definition}[Trivial valuation at a native scheme point]
\label{def:seven-native-trivial-at}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.Berkovich.SchemeValuedPoint.trivialAt}
\leanok
For an actual scheme point $p\in Y$, let $t_p$ be the native valued point
with support $p$ and the trivial valuation on the ACTUAL field $\kappa(p)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Native residue valuation of a binary graded seminorm]
\label{lem:seven-native-binary-residue}
\uses{def:seven-native-trivial-at}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.Berkovich.RelevantGradedSpectrum.nativePoint_residueVal_eq_one_of_binary,
EnvelopeProperty.Berkovich.RelevantGradedSpectrum.nativePoint_eq_trivialAt_of_binary}
\leanok
Let $v$ be a relevant graded seminorm on a graded ring, with every original
ring value in $\{0,1\}$. Its canonical native Proj valued point is
$t_{\operatorname{supp}(v)}$; in particular the ENTIRE valuation of its
true projective stalk residue field is trivial.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every ordinary localization element at the original kernel has the form
$a/s$ with $s$ outside that kernel. Its extended valuation is
$v(a)/v(s)=v(a)$ because $v(s)=1$, and is therefore zero or one. The true
degree-zero homogeneous local valuation is the restriction of this ordinary
localized valuation, so also has only values zero and one. Transport along
the actual projective stalk isomorphism retains that property. Every element
of the true projective residue field is the residue of an actual stalk
element, since the residue map is surjective. Thus its residue valuation has
only values zero and one. A valuation on a field vanishes exactly at zero,
so every nonzero residue has value one. This is the entire trivial valuation
on the actual residue field, not an identification with the affine cone
residue field. The canonical native point already has the original projective
support, proving the whole point equality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The original rank-one support-trivial point is the true native one]
\label{thm:seven-original-native-support-trivial}
\uses{def:seven-original-support-trivial-point, lem:seven-native-binary-residue}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneSupport_supportTrivialPoint,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOnePoint_supportTrivialPoint}
\leanok
Let $\mathcal X$ be the original rank-one model. The original point
$\zeta_x$ has the SAME native Proj support as $x$, and its canonical native
valued point is $t_{\operatorname{supp}(x)}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The proved equality of entire original valuation kernels gives equality
of their actual homogeneous projective prime ideals, hence their native
Proj supports. Every original ring value at $\zeta_x$ is zero or one by
construction. The preceding lemma identifies its canonical native point
with the trivial valuation of the genuine projective residue field at that
support. Replace the support by the equal original native support of $x$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The actual whole analytic homeomorphism preserves support trivialization]
\label{thm:seven-whole-homeomorph-support-trivial}
\uses{thm:seven-original-native-support-trivial}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOne_trivialAt_mem_relativeLocus,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneSupportTrivialRelativePoint,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneAnalyticHomeomorph_supportTrivialPoint}
\leanok
The native valued point $t_{\operatorname{supp}(x)}$ lies over the SAME
original trivial base-field point, and the canonical whole original
analytic homeomorphism maps $\zeta_x$ to that exact native relative point.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The preceding theorem identifies $t_{\operatorname{supp}(x)}$ with the
native point of the genuine original analytic point $\zeta_x$. Such native
points lie over the original trivial base point by the existing canonical
relative comparison. The whole analytic homeomorphism has that SAME original
native valued point as its underlying map; the preceding point equality
therefore proves equality in the actual prescribed-base relative locus.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Actual positive-diagonal transport in every positive rank]
\label{thm:seven-diagonal-native-support-trivial}
\uses{thm:seven-original-native-support-trivial,
thm:seven-whole-homeomorph-support-trivial, lem:gl-veronese}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.veroneseAnMap_supportTrivialPoint,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeDiagonalSupport_supportTrivialPoint,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeDiagonalPoint_supportTrivialPoint,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeDiagonalAnalyticHomeomorph_supportTrivialPoint}
\leanok
Let $\mathcal X$ have positive rank and let $a$ be any strictly positive
integral diagonal degree. Restriction to the SAME original diagonal ring
commutes with support trivialization. Its actual native diagonal Proj
support is unchanged, and its canonical native point and whole original
analytic homeomorphism identify $\zeta_x$ with the genuine trivial residue
valuation at the SAME original diagonal support.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For each original diagonal ring element $p$, its value under restriction
is the value of its image $\psi_a(p)$ in the original multicone ring.
This value is zero before restriction exactly when it is zero afterwards.
Zero scaling therefore gives the same zero-or-one value whether applied
before or after restriction, proving equality of entire original seminorms.
Apply the rank-one native support and whole valued-point comparisons to
this SAME original diagonal model. The canonical diagonal analytic
homeomorphism is the composite of the original restriction homeomorphism
with that model's canonical rank-one native homeomorphism, so the same
point equality proves the entire claimed composite-homeomorphism identity.
\end{proof}

\section{The actual native generic field of the original model}

\begin{lemma}[The original generic support is the actual native generic point]
\label{lem:seven-native-original-generic-point}
\uses{def:gl-model, def:gl-points, thm:seven-original-native-support-trivial}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneSupport_triv_eq_genericPoint,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOnePoint_supportTrivial_triv,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateOpen_nonempty,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGenericPoint_mem_coordinateOpen,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneSupport_eq_genericPoint}
\leanok
Let $Y=\operatorname{Proj}S$ be the actual native scheme of an original
rank-one model, with generic point $\eta$. Every original analytic point of
generic support has native support $\eta$. Every original nonzero coordinate
open contains $\eta$, and is nonempty. The native point of the support-trivial
original trivial analytic point is the trivial valuation of the actual field
$\kappa(\eta)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
An original seminorm of generic support has zero kernel. Its entire actual
native homogeneous prime is therefore zero. The zero homogeneous prime
specializes to every point of the actual projective spectrum, so it is a
generic point; uniqueness of generic points on the integral scheme gives
$\eta$. In particular this applies to the original trivial point. A nonzero
original coordinate has trivial value one, so its native coordinate open
contains that same point and hence $\eta$. The actual support-trivial native
point identity identifies its residue valuation with the trivial valuation
at that support, giving the asserted true generic residue valuation.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The original field and the true native generic field]
\label{def:seven-native-original-field-equivalence}
\uses{def:gl-model, lem:gl-charts, lem:seven-native-original-generic-point}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGenericGerm,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneFunctionFieldEquiv}
\leanok
For every original chart $j$ with nonzero coordinate, let
$\gamma_j:\Gamma(U_j,\mathcal O_Y)\to K(Y)$ be the actual generic germ.
The actual original section-ring isomorphism
$\iota_j:A_j\simeq\Gamma(U_j,\mathcal O_Y)$ extends to a ring equivalence
$e_j:K\simeq K(Y)$.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
The explicit original model generation axiom (M3) gives
$\operatorname{Frac}(A_j)=K$ for the literal inclusion $A_j\subset K$.
The actual native coordinate open is nonempty and affine, so its generic
germ makes $K(Y)$ the fraction field of its actual ring of sections.
Extend $\iota_j$ through those two proved fraction-field structures by the
universal property. This uses (M3); no assertion about arbitrary projective
graded subrings inside $K[T]$ is required.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Every original chart coefficient is its true generic germ]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-field-coefficients}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-field-equivalence}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneFunctionFieldEquiv_coefficient,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneFunctionFieldEquiv_symm_germ}
\leanok
For every $b\in A_j$ and every actual native section
$s\in\Gamma(U_j,\mathcal O_Y)$,
\[
 e_j(b)=\gamma_j(\iota_j(b)),\qquad
 e_j^{-1}(\gamma_j(s))=\iota_j^{-1}(s)\in A_j\subset K.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The fraction-field extension commutes with the original ring maps on every
coefficient, giving the first identity. Apply it to $\iota_j^{-1}(s)$ and
cancel the actual section-ring isomorphism. Applying $e_j^{-1}$ gives the
second identity, for the entire actual section ring.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual native section of an original homogeneous coefficient]
\label{def:seven-native-original-homogeneous-section}
\uses{def:gl-model, lem:gl-charts}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneOriginalHomogeneousSection}
\leanok
For $f\in S_a$, let $s_{j,f}$ be the actual structure-sheaf section on $U_j$
obtained from the native homogeneous fraction $fT^a/z_j^{\deg a}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[The original homogeneous quotients have their actual generic germs]
\label{lem:seven-native-original-homogeneous-germs}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-homogeneous-section,
thm:seven-native-original-field-coefficients}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneOriginalHomogeneousSection_eq,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneFunctionFieldEquiv_homogeneous,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneFunctionFieldEquiv_homogeneous_ratio}
\leanok
Writing $\widetilde z_j^a$ for the original coordinate coefficient denominator,
\[
 s_{j,f}=\iota_j(f/\widetilde z_j^a),\qquad
 e_j(f/\widetilde z_j^a)=\gamma_j(s_{j,f}).
\]
For $f,g\in S_a$ the entire same-degree quotient satisfies
\[
 e_j(f/g)=\gamma_j(s_{j,f})/\gamma_j(s_{j,g}).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The genuine native homogeneous-chart equivalence sends
$fT^a/z_j^{\deg a}$ to the original dehomogenized coefficient
$f/\widetilde z_j^a$. Its inverse followed by the actual homogeneous
section-ring isomorphism is exactly $\iota_j$, proving the first equality.
The exact coefficient theorem gives the second. The original coordinate
denominator is nonzero, and
$(f/\widetilde z_j^a)/(g/\widetilde z_j^a)=f/g$.
Preservation of division by the ring equivalence gives the quotient formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The true generic stalk retains the entire homogeneous fraction]
\label{lem:seven-native-original-homogeneous-stalk}
\uses{lem:seven-native-original-homogeneous-germs,
lem:seven-native-original-generic-point}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGenericGerm_homogeneous_stalk,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGenericGerm_homogeneous_ne_zero}
\leanok
Under the genuine Proj stalk isomorphism at $\eta$, the germ
$\gamma_j(s_{j,f})$ is the image of the same original homogeneous fraction
$fT^a/z_j^{\deg a}$ under the actual map of homogeneous localizations.
If $f\ne0$, this generic germ is nonzero.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The actual structure sheaf evaluates the original homogeneous fraction at
each prime by the localization map to that prime. The genuine Proj stalk
isomorphism sends a germ to precisely this value at $\eta$, proving the
whole fraction identity. For nonzero $f$, the original nonzero coordinate
denominator makes $f/\widetilde z_j^a$ nonzero in $K$. Its image under the
proved field equivalence is the displayed germ and remains nonzero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The original native field equivalence is independent of the chart]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-field-independent}
\uses{lem:seven-native-original-homogeneous-stalk,
lem:seven-native-original-homogeneous-germs, lem:gl-charts}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneFunctionFieldEquiv_independent}
\leanok
For any two original nonzero charts $j,l$, the entire ring equivalences
$e_j,e_l:K\simeq K(Y)$ are equal.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Every $x\in K$ is $f/g$ with $f,g\in S_a$ and $g\ne0$, by (M3) and the
original homogeneous-ratio theorem. The two pairs of actual generic germs
satisfy
$\gamma_j(s_{j,f})\gamma_l(s_{l,g})=
\gamma_l(s_{l,f})\gamma_j(s_{j,g})$.
To see this, transport both products through the genuine Proj stalk
isomorphism at $\eta$ and inject the homogeneous localization into its
ordinary localization. The two literal fractions have the same numerator
$(fT^a)(gT^a)$; their denominators are
$z_j^{\deg a}z_l^{\deg a}$ and $z_l^{\deg a}z_j^{\deg a}$, equal by
commutativity. Injectivity gives the asserted cross product identity in
the actual generic field. Both denominator germs are nonzero by the
preceding lemma. Divide the cross product identity and apply the exact
homogeneous quotient formulas to obtain $e_j(x)=e_l(x)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The whole original valuation is the actual native generic valuation]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-whole-field-valuation}
\uses{lem:seven-native-original-generic-point,
lem:seven-native-original-homogeneous-germs,
thm:seven-native-original-field-independent, def:gl-points}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGenericValuation_homogeneous,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGenericValuation_comap_eq_fieldVal}
\leanok
Let $x$ be an original analytic point of generic support, and let $\nu_x$
be the entire actual native function-field valuation of its canonical
native valued point. Then
\[
 \nu_x(\gamma_j(s_{j,f}))=|fT^a|_x/|z_j|_x^{\deg a},\qquad
 \nu_x\circ e_j=|\cdot|_x\quad\hbox{on all of }K,
\]
where the valuation on the right is the original model field valuation.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The native point has actual support $\eta$. The genuine generic-residue
equivalence identifies the value of every actual local section's generic
germ with its value at that same native point. The already proved native
evaluation of the original homogeneous fraction is
$|fT^a|_x/|z_j|_x^{\deg a}$, giving the first formula. Write any element of
$K$ as $f/g$ with $f,g\in S_a$, using the original homogeneous-ratio theorem.
The exact field quotient formula and multiplicativity of the native
valuation give the quotient of the two displayed homogeneous values.
Generic original support and the original nonzero coordinate make the
common factor $|z_j|_x^{\deg a}$ nonzero, so it cancels. The result is
$|fT^a|_x/|gT^a|_x$, which is exactly the existing original model field
valuation of $f/g$. This proves equality of the whole valuations.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The proved field identification is a ring equivalence. Its exact actual
generic germs and whole generic valuations supply prerequisites for the
intrinsic comparison. Identification of the whole invertible-sheaf section
spaces, their model norms, intrinsic numerical cones and BJ PSH classes
remains separate work.
\end{remark}


\begin{definition}[The original coordinate dual frames]
\label{def:seven-native-original-coordinate-frames}
\uses{def:gl-model, lem:gl-charts}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateIdealGeneratorIso,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateLineOverFrame,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateLineFrame}
\leanok
Let $Y$ be the actual native rank-one model, let $c_i,c_j$ be nonzero
original coordinates, and write $U_i$ for its actual coordinate open.
The actual ideal $I_j$ of the coordinate hyperplane is trivialized on $U_i$
by its actual principal generator $r_{ij}$. Dualizing gives an over-site
frame $e_{ij}:I_j^\vee|_{U_i}\simeq\mathcal O_{U_i}$ and its actual
open-subscheme restriction frame.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
The proved ideal equality on the actual affine open is
$I_j(U_i)=(r_{ij})$, and $r_{ij}$ is a nonzerodivisor because $c_i,c_j$
are nonzero. Multiplication by this generator is therefore the genuine
unit-to-ideal isomorphism. Transport its inverse to the over-site and
apply the actual dual-module functor. Restricting this over-site
isomorphism gives the asserted frame on the actual open subscheme.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual canonical coordinate pole section]
\label{def:seven-native-original-coordinate-pole-section}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-coordinate-frames}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinatePoleUnitHom,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinatePoleSection}
\leanok
The actual ideal inclusion $I_j\hookrightarrow\mathcal O_Y$ dualizes to
$a_j:\mathcal O_Y\to I_j^\vee$. Define the actual global section
$d_j=a_j(1)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The entire canonical map in its true dual frame]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-coordinate-pole-map}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-coordinate-pole-section,
def:seven-native-original-coordinate-frames}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinatePoleUnitHom_frame}
\leanok
On the entire actual over-site of $U_i$, the composite $e_{ij}\circ a_j$
is multiplication by the actual local equation $r_{ij}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
In the principal ideal frame the actual ideal inclusion is multiplication
by $r_{ij}$. Dualize this equality, using the genuine unit-dual
identification and the dual of the produced ideal frame. Evaluation of a
dual map on the original generator gives multiplication by that same
$r_{ij}$ on every object of the over-site, proving the whole morphism
identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Literal original coordinate coefficients of the native equation]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-coordinate-equation}
\uses{thm:seven-native-original-field-coefficients,
lem:seven-native-original-homogeneous-germs}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneHyperplaneLocalEquation_homogeneous,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneHyperplaneLocalEquation_original,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneHyperplaneLocalEquation_generic,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneHyperplaneLocalEquation_ne_zero}
\leanok
The actual local equation is the native homogeneous section of the
original degree-one coefficient $c_j$, and
\[
 r_{ij}=\iota_i(c_j/c_i),\qquad
 \gamma_i(r_{ij})=e_i(c_j/c_i).
\]
Consequently $r_{ij}\ne0$ whenever $c_i,c_j\ne0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The defining native homogeneous fraction is precisely $z_j/z_i$.
The exact original homogeneous-section comparison sends it to $c_j/c_i$
in $A_i$, giving the first identity. The proved entire coefficient/generic
germ identity gives the second. The ratio $c_j/c_i$ is nonzero, and the
proved function-field ring equivalence is injective, so its image and
hence the actual local equation are nonzero.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
These genuine coordinate frames and the entire scalar-map identity are
prerequisites for identifying the whole original saturated section space
with actual native line-bundle global sections. That identification and
its scalar and norm laws remain separate producers.
\end{remark}


\begin{lemma}[Direct actual over-site coefficients of a unit morphism]
\label{lem:seven-native-direct-unit-coefficient}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-coordinate-frames}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.CanonicalGeometry.localFrameCoefficients_unitHom_apply_one}
\leanok
Let $f:\mathcal O_Y\to M$ be an actual module-sheaf morphism and $e$ a
true over-site frame on $U$. If the entire composite $e\circ f|_U$ is
multiplication by $r\in\Gamma(U,\mathcal O_Y)$, the actual regular frame
coefficient of $f(1)|_U$ is $r$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Naturality gives $f(1)|_U=f|_U(1|_U)$, and actual structure-ring restriction
preserves one. Evaluate the entire over-site map identity at its terminal
object and at one. The scalar endomorphism is $1r=r$, and the other side
is precisely the actual regular frame coefficient of the restricted
section. This proves the claimed identity directly on the over-site.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The actual canonical section has its original coordinate coefficient]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-pole-section-coefficient}
\uses{lem:seven-native-direct-unit-coefficient,
thm:seven-native-original-coordinate-pole-map,
thm:seven-native-original-coordinate-equation}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinatePoleSection_coefficient,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinatePoleSection_ne_zero,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinatePoleSection_generic}
\leanok
The actual regular coefficient of $d_j|_{U_i}$ in the produced frame
$e_{ij}$ is $r_{ij}=\iota_i(c_j/c_i)$. Its actual generic frame coefficient
is $e_i(c_j/c_i)$, and the actual global section $d_j$ is nonzero.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Apply the direct coefficient lemma to the proved entire dual inclusion
identity. The exact equation/generic-germ theorem gives the two literal
original coefficient formulas. Model condition (M1) supplies a nonzero
original coordinate chart. On it $r_{ij}\ne0$. A zero global section has
zero restriction and zero frame coefficient, contradicting this
nonvanishing. Thus $d_j\ne0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact intrinsic normalized ratios in the original coordinate frame]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-section-ratio}
\uses{thm:seven-native-original-pole-section-coefficient,
def:seven-native-original-coordinate-frames}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateSectionRatio_formula}
\leanok
For every actual section $s$ of $L_j=I_j^\vee$ on $U_i$, its genuine
intrinsic rational coefficient normalized by the same actual global
section $d_j$ is
\[
 q_s=\gamma_i(e_{ij}(s))/e_i(c_j/c_i).
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The intrinsic section-ratio theorem applies to every true over-site local
bundle frame and computes its normalized ratio as the quotient of the
actual generic coefficients of $s$ and $d_j$. Substitute the just-proved
actual canonical-section generic coefficient into that formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Original coefficient of an actual native global section]
\label{def:seven-native-original-global-section-coefficient}
\uses{thm:seven-native-original-pole-section-coefficient,
def:seven-native-original-field-equivalence}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGlobalSectionCoefficient}
\leanok
For an actual global section $s\in H^0(Y,L_j)$ let $q_s$ be its intrinsic
rational coefficient normalized by $d_j$ and set $f_s=c_j e_i^{-1}(q_s)\in K$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Actual native H0 injects into the full original saturated sections]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-global-section-saturation}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-global-section-coefficient,
thm:seven-native-original-section-ratio,
thm:seven-native-original-field-independent,
thm:seven-native-original-field-coefficients, lem:gl-sections}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGlobalSectionCoefficient_local,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGlobalSectionCoefficient_mem_sections,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGlobalSectionCoefficient_injective}
\leanok
For EVERY original nonzero coordinate chart $U_l$, the entire actual
section $s$ satisfies
\[
 f_s/c_l=\iota_l^{-1}(e_{lj}(s|_{U_l}))\in A_l.
\]
Consequently $f_s$ belongs to the original full saturated degree-one
section space $R_1$, and the map $s\mapsto f_s$ is injective.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Actual restriction preserves the intrinsic rational coefficient. The
proved local ratio formula gives
$q_s=\gamma_l(b_l)/e_l(c_j/c_l)$, where
$b_l=e_{lj}(s|_{U_l})$ is the actual regular frame coefficient. Apply
$e_l^{-1}$ and the entire inverse generic-germ coefficient theorem to get
$e_l^{-1}(q_s)=\iota_l^{-1}(b_l)/(c_j/c_l)$. The entire field equivalence is
independent of the original chart. Since $c_l,c_j\ne0$, field cancellation
gives the displayed equality. Its right side is literally an element of
$A_l$, so the full original saturated chart criterion, for all original
nonzero charts, proves $f_s\in R_1$. If $f_s=f_t$, cancel the nonzero
$c_j$, then use injectivity of $e_i^{-1}$ and genuine intrinsic rational
section injectivity on the nonempty top open to conclude $s=t$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The forward actual H0 map and its full original saturated membership are
proved without a section-space matching premise. Its surjectivity by
actual sheaf gluing and its actual base-field scalar compatibility remain
separate producers. No k-linear equivalence, model norm or BJ PSH
identification is asserted here.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Actual local line sections of full original saturated coefficients]
\label{def:seven-native-original-local-section}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-coordinate-frames,
thm:seven-native-original-section-ratio, lem:gl-sections}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneLocalSectionOfOriginal,

EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneLocalSectionOfOriginal_ratio}

For every ORIGINAL saturated $f\in R_1$ and nonzero original coordinate
chart $U_i$, define the actual local section $s_{i,f}$ of $L_j=I_j^\vee$
by the inverse genuine frame applied to $\iota_i(f/c_i)$. Its whole
regular frame coefficient is exactly $\iota_i(f/c_i)$, and its intrinsic
normalized rational coefficient is $e_i(f/c_j)$.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
The original chart theorem gives $f/c_i\in A_i$. Apply the actual chart-ring
isomorphism, then the inverse of the produced actual regular frame linear
equivalence. Its inverse identities give the entire regular coefficient
law. The exact intrinsic ratio formula and whole original coefficient/germ
comparison give $e_i(f/c_i)/e_i(c_j/c_i)$. The nonzero original coordinate
$c_i$ cancels, giving $e_i(f/c_j)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Genuine native sheaf gluing of all original saturated coefficients]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-global-section-gluing}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-local-section,
thm:seven-native-original-field-independent,
lem:seven-native-original-generic-point, lem:gl-charts}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOne_existsGlobalSectionOfOriginal,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGlobalSectionOfOriginal,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneGlobalSectionOfOriginal_restrict}

For every original $f\in R_1$, the actual local sections $s_{i,f}$ glue to
an actual global section $s_f$ of $L_j$. Its entire restriction on EVERY
nonzero original coordinate chart is precisely $s_{i,f}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The genuine original coordinate cover gives a coordinate avoiding each
homogeneous prime. This original coordinate is nonzero, because zero
belongs to every prime. Thus the nonzero original coordinate charts still
cover the actual scheme. Every pair contains the actual generic point,
so its actual intersection is nonempty. Actual restriction preserves
intrinsic normalized rational coefficients. On an overlap, both restricted
sections have coefficient $e(f/c_j)$ by the preceding lemma and the entire
chart independence of the proved field equivalence. The intrinsic ratio
map is injective on every actual nonempty open, so those whole restricted
sections are equal. The actual module sheaf gluing theorem gives $s_f$
with every asserted restriction; no compatibility or gluing certificate
is supplied as a premise.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The glued section retains the same original saturated coefficient]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-global-section-coefficient-inverse}

def:seven-native-original-global-section-coefficient}

The original global coefficient of the actual glued section $s_f$ is
exactly the SAME original saturated coefficient $f$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Restrict $s_f$ to a nonzero original coordinate chart, where it is exactly
$s_{i,f}$. Restriction preserves the normalized intrinsic ratio, which
is therefore $e_i(f/c_j)$. The definition of the original global coefficient
gives $c_j e_i^{-1}(e_i(f/c_j))=f$, using $c_j\ne0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Full original saturated sections are actual native H0]
\label{thm:seven-native-original-full-H0}
\uses{thm:seven-native-original-global-section-saturation,
thm:seven-native-original-global-section-coefficient-inverse}

The original global coefficient is a bijection
$H^0(Y,L_j)\simeq R_1$. Its forward map is literally $s\mapsto f_s$,
and its inverse is exactly the actual section $f\mapsto s_f$ constructed
by genuine native sheaf gluing.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The original global coefficient is injective and lands in the original
FULL saturated section space by the preceding checked forward theorem.
The just-proved coefficient identity for $s_f$ proves surjectivity onto
that same original space. Form the resulting bijection. Its forward map
is its defining original coefficient, and injectivity plus the coefficient
identity forces its inverse to be exactly the glued section $s_f$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This is a bijection with exact entire actual local coefficient laws.
Actual base-field scalar compatibility, tensor powers, model norms,
intrinsic numerical cones and BJ PSH classes remain further producers.
No k-linear or model-norm identification is included in this statement.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[The genuine native structure-induced base actions]
\label{def:seven-native-actual-base-actions}

Its actual global constant homomorphism is
$\kappa=(p^*)\circ(\Gamma(\operatorname{Spec}k)\simeq k)^{-1}$.
The actual function field has k-algebra map $\gamma_\top\circ\kappa$.
Actual H0 carries the k-module action obtained by restricting its TRUE
structure-sheaf global-function action along $\kappa$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The entire actual native base map retains original chart coefficients]
\label{thm:seven-native-actual-base-chart-law}
\uses{def:seven-native-actual-base-actions,
thm:seven-native-original-field-coefficients}

For every nonzero original chart, the ENTIRE actual base-constant map
satisfies $\operatorname{res}_{U_i}\circ\kappa=\iota_i\circ(k\to A_i)$.
In particular $\kappa(a)|_{U_i}=\iota_i(a)$ for EVERY original $a\in k$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The true original scheme chart isomorphism
$U_i\simeq\operatorname{Spec}A_i$ already commutes with the ACTUAL structure
morphisms over k. Apply true global-section pullback to that entire scheme
morphism equality. Actual Spec/global-section naturality identifies the
base pullback with the original coefficient homomorphism $k\to A_i$.
The actual chart pullback followed by the actual open/top section
isomorphism is exactly the proved original section-ring isomorphism
$\iota_i$. The open inclusion's global-section pullback followed by that
same open/top isomorphism is actual restriction. These genuine entire
ring-map identities give the asserted whole map equality. Evaluate it at
the arbitrary original coefficient $a$ for the elementwise law.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The original field equivalence is an actual base-algebra equivalence]
\label{thm:seven-native-actual-function-field-algebra}
\uses{thm:seven-native-actual-base-chart-law,

EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneFunctionFieldAlgEquiv}

$e_i(a)=\gamma_\top(\kappa(a))$ for EVERY original $a\in k$.
It therefore promotes to $K\simeq_k K(Y)$ for the ACTUAL
structure-morphism-induced native function-field algebra.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The entire original coefficient/generic-germ theorem gives
$e_i(a)=\gamma_i(\iota_i(a))$ for the original chart coefficient inclusion.
The preceding actual restriction identity makes this
$\gamma_i(\kappa(a)|_{U_i})$. True generic germs commute with actual
restriction, so this is $\gamma_\top(\kappa(a))$.
That is exactly commutation of the proved ring equivalence with the two
ACTUAL base scalar maps, proving the asserted algebra equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Literal additivity and actual base-scalar compatibility of native coefficients]
\label{thm:seven-native-actual-H0-scalar-laws}

For EVERY actual global section and EVERY original $a\in k$,

 f_{s+t}=f_s+f_t,\qquad f_{\kappa(a)\cdot s}=a f_s.
\]
The scalar on the left is the ACTUAL structure-sheaf global-function action.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The genuine intrinsic ratio map is additive and semilinear along the true
structure-sheaf generic germ. Additivity of the inverse original ring
equivalence and multiplication by $c_j$ give the additive identity.
Actual semilinearity gives
$q_{\kappa(a)\cdot s}=\gamma_\top(\kappa(a))q_s=e_i(a)q_s$.
Apply $e_i^{-1}$ and multiply by the original $c_j$. Commutativity in the
ORIGINAL field K gives precisely the displayed scalar equality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Full original saturated sections are actual native H0 linearly over the base]
\label{thm:seven-native-actual-linear-full-H0}

Its forward map remains literally $s\mapsto f_s$, and its inverse is the

\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The preceding literal additive and actual scalar laws make the already

\begin{remark}
These actual scalar identifications concern the original rank-one native

\subsection{Original coordinates give actual global generators on the native cover}
Throughout this subsection $X$ is the original rank-one model, $Y$ its actual
native scheme, and $c_j\ne0$ is an original coordinate. The line $L_j$ is the
actual dual hyperplane-ideal line. All assertions hold over the original
field without an algebraic closedness hypothesis.

\begin{lemma}[The actual whole section map in an over-site frame]
\label{lem:seven-native-whole-section-map-frame}
\uses{def:seven-native-original-coordinate-frames}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.CanonicalGeometry.sectionHom_over_comp_frame,
EnvelopeProperty.CanonicalGeometry.sectionHom_over_eq_frame_inv}
\leanok
For any integral scheme, actual module sheaf M, actual over-site frame
$e:M|_{/U}\simeq\mathcal O_{/U}$, and global section s, let r be the
coefficient of its actual restriction to U. The SAME actual unit-to-module
section morphism satisfies $\alpha_s|_{/U}\circ e=r\,\mathrm{id}$.
If r=1, the whole morphism $\alpha_s|_{/U}$ is exactly $e^{-1}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
An endomorphism of the unit on the over-site is determined by its value at
one at the terminal object U. The actual global-section/unit-map adjunction
sends one to the true restriction of s. Applying e therefore gives r,
which is also the value of the actual scalar endomorphism. This proves the
whole morphism equality. In the case r=1, cancellation of the invertible
e gives the inverse-frame identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual original coordinate global sections]
\label{def:seven-native-coordinate-global-sections}
\uses{thm:seven-native-original-global-section-gluing,
thm:seven-native-original-global-section-coefficient-inverse}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneOriginalCoordinateSection,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateGlobalSection,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateGlobalSection_coefficient,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateGlobalSection_local,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateGlobalUnitHom}
\leanok
Every original coordinate $c_t$ lies in the ORIGINAL full saturated $R_1$.
Define $w_t\in H^0(Y,L_j)$ to be its actual sheaf-glued inverse under the
proved original H0 comparison, and define $\alpha_t:\mathcal O_Y\to L_j$
by the actual global-section/unit-map adjunction. Its true original global
coefficient is literally $c_t$. On every original nonzero chart i the true
frame coefficient of $w_t|_{U_i}$ is the actual chart section corresponding
to $c_t/c_i$.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
The original degree-one piece contains $c_t$ by construction and embeds into
the original full $R_1$ by the original saturation lemma. The previously proved
actual sheaf-gluing inverse produces $w_t$. Its exact global coefficient is
$c_t$ by the true inverse law, while its proved exact chart restriction is the
actual inverse frame applied to the literal original regular ratio $c_t$/$c_i$.
Applying this same frame gives the claimed local coefficient.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Original coordinates are genuine local native generators]
\label{thm:seven-native-coordinate-local-generators}
\uses{lem:seven-native-whole-section-map-frame,
def:seven-native-coordinate-global-sections}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateGlobalUnitHom_frame,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateGlobalUnitHom_self,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOne_existsCoordinateGlobalGenerator}
\leanok
On every original nonzero chart i, the whole unit map $\alpha_t$ has the
actual scalar $c_t/c_i$ in the produced true coordinate frame. In particular
$\alpha_{i}|_{/U_i}=e_{i,j}^{-1}$ as whole original sheaf morphisms.
Every actual native point lies in an original nonzero coordinate chart on
which the corresponding actual original coordinate global morphism is an
isomorphism.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:seven-native-whole-section-map-frame} to the actual
coefficients of Definition~\ref{def:seven-native-coordinate-global-sections}.
For the self-coordinate, $c_i/c_i=1$ since $c_i$ is nonzero, and the actual
chart-ring isomorphism preserves one. This gives the whole inverse-frame
identity. The actual original coordinate opens cover native Proj. If a
point belongs to $D_+$($c_t$), then $c_t$ cannot be zero: its homogeneous element
would be zero and hence belong to that point's actual homogeneous prime,
contradicting membership in $D_+$($c_t$). In rank one t therefore defines an
original nonzero chart i. The proved inverse-frame identity makes the
actual corresponding coordinate morphism invertible there.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The same original local generators under actual pullback]
\label{lem:seven-native-coordinate-pullback-generators}
\uses{thm:seven-native-coordinate-local-generators}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.CanonicalGeometry.isIso_restrict_of_isIso_over,
EnvelopeProperty.CanonicalGeometry.isIso_pullback_of_isIso_over,
EnvelopeProperty.MultiModel.nativeRankOneCoordinateGlobalUnitHom_pullback_isIso}
\leanok
A whole original module morphism which is invertible on an over-site is
invertible after the actual open-subscheme restriction and actual sheaf
pullback to that open. In particular the SAME original coordinate unit
map $\alpha_i$ is invertible after actual pullback to $U_i$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The actual over-site/open-subscheme equivalence takes the over-site map
to the true restriction map, up to the proved natural isomorphism. A functor
preserves isomorphisms, and conjugation by a natural isomorphism preserves
the property of being an isomorphism. Apply next the genuine natural
restriction/pullback isomorphism. The preceding theorem discharges the
actual original coordinate case, with no local generation premise supplied.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The theorem produces actual coordinate global morphisms which are invertible
on a true cover; it does not yet package the finite free evaluation map as
an epimorphism. The model-norm consumer can already use these actual local

 N_{p,\mathbb R}=\mathbb R\otimes_{\mathbb Z}N_p,
 \qquad \iota_p(a)=1\otimes a.
\]
This is the actual real vector space obtained by extending scalars from
the original native numerical quotient, with its canonical additive map.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
The original commutative numerical group is canonically a module over
the integers. The actual tensor product over the integers therefore
exists, with real scalar action on its real factor. Its canonical map
is additive by the defining tensor relations, so original Picard tensor
product is carried to addition of the corresponding real classes.
\end{proof}

module morphisms $\mathcal O_Y\to(f^*M)^\vee$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By the proved whole unit-Hom extensionality it suffices to evaluate at
the literal section $1$ on the top open. Actual adjunction-unit
naturality for the entire original map $s_q$ identifies pullback of its
value at $1$ with its actual pulled map evaluated on the actual
adjunction image of $1$. The true pullback-unit adjunction formula
identifies that image with inverse pullback-unit applied to the literal
$f^\sharp(1)=1$. The proved canonical dual-section evaluation identifies
$s_q(1)$ with the actual original functional $q$ restricted to the top
open. Apply the SAME actual dualPullbackHom component. Its genuine
adjunction-unit component theorem gives localDualPullback of that actual
restricted functional. The proved whole-functional pullback law identifies
this with the actual restriction of $q_f$. Finally the canonical
$s_{q_f}$ evaluated at $1$ is exactly that SAME functional by the proved
evaluation theorem. The inverse image of the top open is top; the whole
unit-Hom extensionality now gives the claimed entire equality. No scalar,
frame, section or norm normalization is an input.
\end{proof}

For the original actual model, apply this theorem to the actual native
and ambient hyperplane ideal inclusions. The already PROVED whole
inclusion equality for the SAME earlier five-factor ideal iso identifies
the two actual pulled functionals. Contravariant dual functoriality,
the actual ideal-iso inverse law and the SAME two canonical dual-pullback
isos then give literal original/native coordinate-pole section transport.
This remains a subsequent checked consumer, not an assumed matching datum.

\subsection{Actual dual comparison retains canonical sections of whole functionals}
\begin{lemma}[Canonical dual sections under an actual module iso]
\label{lem:seven-dual-canonical-section-iso}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.OriginalDualPullbackCoherence.canonicalDualSection_iso}
\leanok
Let $e:A\simeq B$ be an actual module iso on a scheme $Z$, and let
$q_A:A\to\mathcal O_Z$ and $q_B:B\to\mathcal O_Z$ be actual whole
functionals satisfying the whole morphism equality $q_B\circ e=q_A$.
Then the inverse actual dual iso sends the canonical dual section of
$q_A$ to the canonical dual section of $q_B$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The actual inverse-iso law gives $q_A\circ e^{-1}=q_B$.
The genuine contravariant dual composition law therefore identifies
$q_A^\vee$ followed by the inverse dual iso with $q_B^\vee$.
Precompose by the SAME actual inverse dual-unit iso. No section matching
equation is an input; the input is an ordinary whole functional square.
\end{proof}
\begin{theorem}[Canonical actual pulled-dual comparison preserves the section]
\label{thm:seven-pulled-dual-comparison-canonical-section}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.OriginalDualPullbackCoherence.pulledDualComparison_section}
\leanok
\uses{thm:seven-canonical-dual-section-pullback,lem:seven-dual-canonical-section-iso}
Let $f_N:Z\to Y_N$ and $f_P:Z\to Y_P$ be actual scheme morphisms, and
$I_N,I_P$ actual invertible modules with actual functionals $q_N,q_P$
to their structure modules. Let $e:f_N^*I_N\simeq f_P^*I_P$ be an
actual module iso preserving the true pulled functionals after the
actual pullback-unit maps. The literal comparison consisting of the
actual native dual-pullback iso, inverse actual dual of $e$, and inverse
actual other-side dual-pullback iso sends the true pulled canonical
$q_N$ section to the true pulled canonical $q_P$ section.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Postcompose both whole section morphisms by the actual other-side dual
pullback iso, which cancels its actual inverse. The proved SAME canonical
dual-section pullback theorem gives the canonical section of each
literal pulled functional. The preceding actual module-iso dual law
identifies those two sections from the given whole functional square.
Cancel the genuine other-side iso. All dual, unit and pullback maps are
their original canonical maps; no frame or section comparison is assumed.
\end{proof}
The original projective application supplies this whole functional square
INTERNALLY from the already proved inclusion law for the SAME earlier
frozen five-factor ideal iso. It does not add that square as a hypothesis.

\subsection{The SAME old H0 presentation and actual section-Hom map}
This route is independent of named coordinate-pole normalization.
\begin{lemma}[Actual whole section under the restriction/pullback comparison]
\label{lem:seven-original-open-pullback-section-restriction}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.ClosedProjectiveGlobalSections.pullbackSectionHom_comp_restrictComparison}
\leanok
Let $f:Z\to Y$ be a genuine scheme open immersion, $L$ an actual
$\mathcal O_Y$-module, and $\alpha:\mathcal O_Y\to L$ an actual whole
section morphism. Its actual unit-normalized pullback followed by the
inverse actual restriction/pullback comparison equals the inverse actual
restriction-unit iso followed by literal restriction of $\alpha$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $t_L:f_{\rm res}^*L\simeq f^*L$ for the actual natural
restriction/pullback comparison, $u_f:f^*\mathcal O_Y\simeq\mathcal O_Z$
and $r_f:f_{\rm res}^*\mathcal O_Y\simeq\mathcal O_Z$.
Naturality of the SAME actual $t$ gives
$f^*\alpha\,t_L^{-1}=t_{\mathcal O}^{-1}f_{\rm res}^*\alpha$.
The existing genuine unit theorem is
$t_{\mathcal O}u_f=r_f$; cancellation gives
$u_f^{-1}t_{\mathcal O}^{-1}=r_f^{-1}$.
Precompose naturality by $u_f^{-1}$ and substitute this whole unit
identity. These are whole morphism equalities, before evaluation on any
section, and require no invertibility of $L$ or properness of $f$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The SAME original top-section iso evaluates actual whole pullback}
\begin{theorem}[Forward top-section coherence]
\label{thm:seven-iso-top-section-forward-coherence}
\uses{lem:seven-original-open-pullback-section-restriction}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.ClosedProjectiveGlobalSections.isoPullbackTopSections_hom_pullbackSectionHom}
\leanok
For every genuine scheme iso $e:Z\simeq Y$, actual module $L$ on $Y$
and whole section $\alpha:\mathcal O_Y\to L$, the original frozen
$\operatorname{isoPullbackTopSections}(e,L)$ sends the actual top
value at $1$ of the genuine unit-normalized pullback of $\alpha$ to
the original top value $\alpha(1)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The proved whole open restriction/pullback comparison applies to $e$.
Evaluate it at the original global unit $1$. The actual inverse
restriction-unit component is the original inverse ring map on the
image open, hence sends $1$ to $1$. Literal restriction of $\alpha$
therefore gives its value at $1$ on that same original image open.
The image of the whole open under the genuine iso $e$ is the whole
original target. Naturality of $\alpha$ along this actual equality of
opens and preservation of $1$ by the actual restriction map give the
original whole value $\alpha(1)$. The three actual factors just used
are exactly those in the frozen top-section iso: inverse comparison,
original restriction-app iso and original equality-induced restriction.
No different H0 presentation or matching equation is introduced.
\end{proof}
\begin{theorem}[Inverse top-section coherence]
\label{thm:seven-iso-top-section-inverse-coherence}
\uses{thm:seven-iso-top-section-forward-coherence}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.ClosedProjectiveGlobalSections.isoPullbackTopSections_inv_sectionAtOne}
\leanok
The inverse SAME top-section iso sends $\alpha(1)$ to the actual top
value of the genuine unit-normalized pullback of $\alpha$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Apply the genuine forward top-section iso to both sides. Its actual
inverse law reduces the first side to $\alpha(1)$; the preceding
forward coherence reduces the second side to the same value. Cancel
the actual iso. The result uses no properness, invertibility,
nonemptiness, base field or coordinate assumptions.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Actual top-section coherence through a whole line-module iso}
\begin{theorem}[Whole top-section and line comparison]
\label{thm:seven-iso-line-top-section-value-coherence}
\uses{thm:seven-iso-top-section-inverse-coherence}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.ClosedProjectiveGlobalSections.isoLineTopSections_sectionAtOne}
\leanok
Let $e:Z\simeq Y$ be a genuine scheme iso, $L$ an actual module on
$Y$, $P$ an actual module on $Z$ and $a:e^*L\simeq P$ an actual
whole module iso. The composite of the inverse SAME original
$\operatorname{isoPullbackTopSections}(e,L)$ with the top component of
$a$ sends $\alpha(1)$ to the actual top value at $1$ of genuine
unit-normalized pullback of $\alpha$ followed by $a$, for every whole
$\alpha:\mathcal O_Y\to L$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The actual inverse top-section coherence identifies its first factor
with the genuine pulled whole section evaluated at $1$. Applying the
actual top component of $a$ gives exactly evaluation of whole
postcomposition at $1$. This calculation is made for arbitrary
schemes, modules and the actual whole iso $a$; it does not expand the
geometry of any concrete original projective model.
\end{proof}
\begin{theorem}[Whole section-Hom coherence through the same line comparison]
\label{thm:seven-iso-line-top-section-hom-coherence}
\uses{thm:seven-iso-line-top-section-value-coherence}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.ClosedProjectiveGlobalSections.isoLineTopSections_topSectionHom}
\leanok
For every actual global section $s$ of $L$, the actual top-section Hom
of its image under that SAME composite equals the genuine whole
unit-normalized pullback of the actual top-section Hom of $s$, followed
by the SAME whole module map $a$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Both whole maps from the actual structure module are determined by
their actual top value at $1$. The actual top-section Hom construction
has value $s$ there. Apply the preceding value identity to that
actual top-section Hom. It identifies the two top values, hence the
two actual whole morphisms. No H0/Hom, frame, coefficient, norm or
section-matching certificate is input; $a$ is the actual ordinary
module iso. Neither module needs invertibility or properness.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The SAME original coordinate H0 and whole section-Hom presentations}
\begin{theorem}[Actual original H0 on whole section values]
\label{thm:seven-original-coordinate-h0-hom-values}
\uses{thm:seven-iso-line-top-section-value-coherence}
\lean{EnvelopeProperty.ClosedProjectiveGenericCoefficients.fixedOriginalNativeCoordinateH0Iso_sectionAtOne}
\leanok
Let $i:X\hookrightarrow\mathbf P^n_k$ be the genuine original integral

\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Use the genuine original/native scheme iso and the SAME native

Postcompose with the top component of the SAME earlier coordinate-line

\end{proof}
